# ferrum-state API matrix

> What: status of every cm6 export and Ferrum public item (state audit). Versions: cm6 `@codemirror/state` 6.7.6 (`mirrors/cm6/state/package.json`); Ferrum line numbers at commit `fb99ca1` (`crates/` unchanged through `ed70610`). Ids: id schemes and path rules in `docs/parity/README.md`.

One row per cm6 export row of `docs/parity/state/raw/a1-cm6-exports.md` (264, of which 2 are `DROP`) and per Ferrum public item of `docs/parity/state/raw/a2-ferrum-pub.md` (391). Statuses are **proposals** for the human gate.

Columns `evidence` name the raw file that holds the full row, the behavior ids, the reproductions (ids `R…`, `P…`, `S…` refer to that file's `## Reproductions` section) and the search commands for `MISSING`. Text in *notes* is the evidence summary from that file; where the main session changed a subagent's proposal (all `candidate:` rows, plus the rows listed in the decision column), the decision and its justification are in the *main-session decision* column. Behaviors per item: `docs/parity/state/behaviors.md`. Tests: `docs/parity/state/tests-side-by-side.md`.

Position unit: every position/length in this crate is a `char` (code point) index in Ferrum and a UTF-16 code unit in cm6 (D1); unless a row says otherwise, ASCII-only reproductions give identical numbers.

## Counts

cm6 rows: 264. `PORTED, DEVIATION` 139, `MISSING` 78, `PORTED, RUST-IDIOMATIC` 22, `PORTED` 15, `HUMAN DECISION` 4, `BLOCKED: D1` 3, `— (DROP)` 2, `PLACEMENT DEVIATION` 1.

Ferrum rows with their own status: 323. `PORTED, DEVIATION` 155, `HUMAN DECISION` 59, `FERRUM-ONLY` 40, `PORTED, RUST-IDIOMATIC` 31, `PORTED` 22, `BLOCKED: D1` 7, `PLACEMENT DEVIATION` 6, `BLOCKED: D2` 3.

Ferrum re-export rows (status inherited from the target row): 68. `re-export → PORTED, DEVIATION` 49, `re-export → HUMAN DECISION` 9, `re-export → PORTED, RUST-IDIOMATIC` 4, `re-export → PLACEMENT DEVIATION` 3, `re-export → PORTED` 2, `(re-export)` 1.

Total rows: 264 + 391 = 655.

No cm6 row is `PORTED` unless every behavior in `docs/parity/state/behaviors.md` is implemented and tested in Ferrum.

| state.cm6.035 | `StateCommand` | `mirrors/cm6/state/src/extension.ts:10` | CORE | `crates/ferrum-commands/src/commands.rs:20` `pub type StateCommand = fn(&EditorState) -> Option<TransactionSpec>`; re-exported `crates/ferrum-commands/src/lib.rs:26`, facade `crates/ferrum/src/lib.rs:25`. Nothing in `ferrum-state` (searched `StateCommand` in all crates). | **PLACEMENT DEVIATION** | Defined only in `crates/ferrum-commands/src/commands.rs:20` with signature `fn(&EditorState) -> Option<TransactionSpec>` (cm6: `(target:{state,dispatch}) => boolean`, `mirrors/cm6/state/src/extension.ts:10`). Signature is D5. See `docs/parity/state/placement.md`, XC-001. | state.StateCommand.B1–B5 | none / none (no cm6 state test; no ferrum-state test) | D5 | cm6: `(target: {state, dispatch}) => boolean`, a closure type defined in `@codemirror/state`. Ferrum: a plain `fn` pointer in `ferrum-commands` that returns a spec instead of dispatching. Differences: (1) placement (commands, not state); (2) no `dispatch`, so a command cannot dispatch 0 or n>1 transactions; (3) `Option<TransactionSpec>` replaces `boolean`, so "handled, nothing dispatched" (`true` without dispatch) cannot be expressed except as `Some(empty spec)`; (4) `fn` pointer cannot capture an environment, cm6 commands are arbitrary closures. repro: type-level, no runtime repro (no Ferrum counterpart in ferrum-state). | `docs/parity/state/raw/C4-api.md` |

Every Ferrum test ignores the open counts. | `docs/parity/state/raw/C6-api.md` |
